I know they have to do what's best for the casino. Is it normal for something like a schedule change to affect your appetite like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about food or your weight when you're not eating?\n\nPatient: Um, no, honestly I don't. I really hope I'm not sounding like a broken record, but it just doesn't cross my mind much at all. When I'm not eating, I'm usually just trying to focus on my shift in the surveillance room, making sure I'm doing a good job, you know? \n\nI definitely don't sit around obsessing over calories or the scale or anything like that. I'd hate for you to think I was hiding some sort of fixation on it. It's really just that food is sort of... an afterthought right now. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer. I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of what's going on. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Would you estimate that you've lost more than 5% of your body weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think it's been that much, honestly. I haven't been weighing myself or anything, so I can't say for sure, but my uniform still fits mostly the same. Maybe just a tiny bit looser, but nothing crazy. \n\nLike I was saying, I really just came in because for the last few weeks I've barely had an appetite. Even the foods I usually like after a long shift, I just sit there and kind of force down a few bites. It's just not appealing right now. \n\nI really hope that doesn't sound like I'm letting myself go or not taking care of myself. I'm trying my best to keep my strength up for work. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy or, you know, wasting my free time. I always try to stay active and keep busy when I'm not at the casino. 

But lately... I guess I've just been a little off my routine. I usually really look forward to my morning runs, and I used to play my guitar a lot, or spend time cooking a nice dinner. But recently, I don't know, I keep skipping them. It's not that I don't want to be productive! It's just... they almost feel like chores right now, and I just can't seem to get myself to care about doing them the way I used to. 

I really don't want you to think I'm just giving up on my hobbies or being ungrateful for my time off. I'm sure it's just a phase and I'll bounce back soon. Is it normal to feel that way when your routine changes at work? I hope I'm explaining this okay for you.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, I haven't had to buy any new clothes or anything like that. Like I mentioned before, my uniform still fits pretty much the same. I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. 

The main thing, like I was saying, is just that my appetite has been so low lately. For the past few weeks, I just haven't felt like eating. Even when I get home from a long shift and try to make something I normally really enjoy, I just end up staring at it and forcing myself to take a few bites. 

I really don't want it to seem like I'm starving myself on purpose or doing anything reckless. I know how important it is to eat right, especially with my job. I'm really trying my best to keep eating, it's just... hard right now. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question the way you need me to.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, I guess it's been about... maybe three weeks now? Right around the time the casino did a big reorganization of our security operations. They, um, they changed up my assignments and my schedule a bit. 

I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining about my job or anything. I know I'm lucky to have it, and I always want to be a team player. It's just that since the changes, I've just been feeling a little off my game, I suppose. 

I've been trying to force myself to get back out there for my runs, and I keep telling myself I'll pick up the guitar this weekend, but... I don't know, the energy just hasn't been there. I'm really trying not to let it get the best of me, though. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline for all this.

I just wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by getting it checked out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I'm sitting around crying or anything like that, so I don't want to make it sound worse than it is. Like I mentioned when we first started talking, it's really just been these last few weeks where I've barely had an appetite and have just been forcing down those few bites after my shifts. \n\nSince it hasn't been going on for a super long time, I probably don't look all that different to my family or the guys at work. I really hope I'm not making too much of it... I just figured I should bring it up since, you know, even the foods I usually like are just sitting there on the plate untouched. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for. I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down and the loss of appetite been affecting your ability to work or interact with your family?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to say no, because I'm still showing up for my shifts and getting the basics done, you know? I don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything. I'm trying really hard to keep everything normal.\n\nBut... well, I guess if I'm being completely honest, it has been a little tough lately. I've made a few documentation mistakes on my logs, and... I actually missed a surveillance cue the other day. It wasn't anything huge, but I did get a formal warning for it. It's just so embarrassing to even admit that, because I've always been really good at my job. It's just... it's starting to affect things in a way it never used to, with this heavy feeling and not eating. \n\nI hope it's okay that I'm telling you this. I just want to be honest so you can help me. Does that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you been thinking a lot about your weight or how you look lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight on purpose or worrying about how I look. I just try to look neat and professional for work, of course. \n\nI hope that's okay to say. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no. No, definitely not. I just don't want you to think I'm doing this on purpose or feeling weird about the food itself. Does that make sense? I hope that answers what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific happened recently that you think might be contributing to your loss of appetite?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, it's fine, I'm handling it, I really am. I don't want you to think I can't handle a little change. It's just... I think maybe the stress of the new routine kind of threw me off, and that's when I stopped feeling hungry. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm making excuses or blaming the job. I actually missed a surveillance cue the other day. It wasn't anything huge, but I did get a formal warning for it. It's just so embarrassing to even admit that, because I've always been really good at my job. It's just... it's starting to affect things in a way it never used to, with this heavy feeling and not eating. \n\nI hope it's okay that I'm telling you this. I just want to be honest so you can help me. Does that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you been thinking a lot about your weight or how you look lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I hope it doesn't seem like I'm, you know, obsessed with my appearance or anything like that. I've never really been the type to spend a lot of time in front of the mirror. \n\nIt's really just the clothes fitting a little looser because of the appetite thing, like I mentioned before. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight on purpose or worrying about how I look. I just try to look neat and professional for work, of course. \n\nI hope that's okay to say. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no. No, definitely not. I hope it doesn't sound like I have some sort of eating problem or anything like that. I really don't. \n\nLike I said, it's just that the food doesn't sound good, and I'm just forcing it down so I don't pass out at work or something. But when I do manage to eat, I don't feel bad about it afterward. I actually feel a little relieved that I got something in my stomach, you know? \n\nI really hope I'm explaining this right. I just don't want you to think I'm doing this on purpose or feeling weird about the food itself. Does that make sense? I hope that answers what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific happened recently that you think might be contributing to your loss of appetite?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining about work, because I know I'm lucky to have my job. I'm usually very adaptable, you know? I always try to be a team player. \n\nBut, I guess... well, this whole thing started about three weeks ago. The casino reorganized our security operations and, um, they changed my assignments around. I mean, it's fine, I'm handling it, I really am. I don't want you to think I can't handle a little change. It's just... I think maybe the stress of the new routine kind of threw me off, and that's when I stopped feeling hungry. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm making excuses or blaming the job. I know they have to do what's best for the casino. Is it normal for something like a schedule change to affect your appetite like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about food or your weight when you're not eating?\n\nPatient: Um, no, honestly I don't. I really hope I'm not sounding like a broken record, but it just doesn't cross my mind much at all. When I'm not eating, I'm usually just trying to focus on my shift in the surveillance room, making sure I'm doing a good job, you know? \n\nI definitely don't sit around obsessing over calories or the scale or anything like that. I'd hate for you to think I was hiding some sort of fixation on it. It's really just that food is sort of... an afterthought right now. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer. I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of what's going on. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Would you estimate that you've lost more than 5% of your body weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think it's been that much, honestly. I haven't been weighing myself or anything, so I can't say for sure, but my uniform still fits mostly the same. Maybe just a tiny bit looser, but nothing crazy. \n\nLike I was saying, I really just came in because for the last few weeks I've barely had an appetite. Even the foods I usually like after a long shift, I just sit there and kind of force down a few bites. It's just not appealing right now. \n\nI really hope that doesn't sound like I'm letting myself go or not taking care of myself. I'm trying my best to keep my strength up for work. Does that sound like it could be a 5% loss? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any pleasure or interest in activities you normally enjoy outside of work?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy or, you know, wasting my free time. I always try to stay active and keep busy when I'm not at the casino. \n\nBut lately... I guess I've just been a little off my routine. I usually really look forward to my morning runs, and I used to play my guitar a lot, or spend time cooking a nice dinner. But recently, I don't know, I keep skipping them. It's not that I don't want to be productive! It's just... they almost feel like chores right now, and I just can't seem to get myself to care about doing them the way I used to. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm just giving up on my hobbies or being ungrateful for my time off. I'm sure it's just a phase and I'll bounce back soon. Is it normal to feel that way when your routine changes at work? I hope I'm explaining this okay for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight, such as having to buy smaller clothes?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't had to buy any new clothes or anything like that. I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. \n\nThe main thing, like I was saying, is just that my appetite has been so low lately. For the past few weeks, I just haven't felt like eating. Even when I get home from a long shift and try to make something I normally really enjoy, I just end up staring at it and forcing myself to take a few bites. \n\nI really don't want it to seem like I'm starving myself on purpose or doing anything reckless. I know how important it is to eat right, especially with my job. I'm really trying my best to keep eating, it's just... hard right now. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your body shape or weight when you're not trying to eat or think about food?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I hope it doesn't sound like I'm obsessed with my appearance or anything like that. I'm really not. I'm just a regular guy, you know? \n\nI don't spend a lot of time analyzing my body shape or worrying about my weight when I'm just going about my day or working my shifts. I try to stay focused on my job and being professional. \n\nIt's really just the eating part that's been a struggle lately, like I was saying. The thought of food just isn't there, but I'm definitely not fixating on how I look in the mirror or trying to lose weight on purpose. I know that wouldn't be healthy. \n\nI hope that's a good answer for you. Is that what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about harming yourself lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. Definitely not.
